### MIRA: A Novel Framework for Fusing Modalities in Medical RAG
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07902v1)
- **Authors**: Jinhong Wang, Tajamul Ashraf, Zongyan Han, Jorma Laaksonen, Rao Mohammad Anwer
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have significantly advanced AI-assisted medical diagnosis, but they often generate factually inconsistent responses that deviate from established medical knowledge. Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) enhances factual accuracy by integrating external sources, but it presents two key challenges. First, insufficient retrieval can miss critical information, whereas excessive retrieval can introduce irrelevant or misleading content, disrupting model output. Second, even when the model initially provides correct answers, over-reliance on retrieved data can lead to factual errors. To address these issues, we introduce the Multimodal Intelligent Retrieval and Augmentation (MIRA) framework, designed to optimize factual accuracy in MLLM. MIRA consists of two key components: (1) a calibrated Rethinking and Rearrangement module that dynamically adjusts the number of retrieved contexts to manage factual risk, and (2) A medical RAG framework integrating image embeddings and a medical knowledge base with a query-rewrite module for efficient multimodal reasoning. This enables the model to effectively integrate both its inherent knowledge and external references. Our evaluation of publicly available medical VQA and report generation benchmarks demonstrates that MIRA substantially enhances factual accuracy and overall performance, achieving new state-of-the-art results. Code is released at https://github.com/mbzuai-oryx/MIRA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MIRA, a novel Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) framework designed to improve the accuracy and reliability of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in medical applications, specifically Visual Question Answering (VQA). MIRA addresses two key challenges: retrieval imbalance (over-reliance on specific data sources) and over-reliance on retrieved information, which can lead to factual errors. The framework incorporates:

1.  **Calibrated Rethinking and Rearrangement (RTRA):** A module dynamically adjusts the amount of retrieved context to manage factual risk using iterative "rethink-rearrange" cycles.
2.  **Multimodal RAG (MRAG):** Integrates image embeddings and a medical knowledge base, enhanced with a query-rewrite module for efficient multimodal reasoning, combining both offline and online (real-time web sources) retrieval.
3.  A dynamic attention mechanism for aligning and fusing visual and textual modalities based on the query type.

The authors evaluate MIRA on publicly available medical VQA and report generation benchmarks (MIMIC-CXR and PubMedVision), demonstrating substantial improvements in factual accuracy and overall performance, achieving state-of-the-art results and claiming a 9x speedup compared to a 72B parameter baseline model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the *integrated and dynamic approach to RAG in the medical domain*. While RAG itself is not new, MIRA's combination of RTRA, MRAG with both offline/online retrieval, and adaptive modality fusion differentiates it. The idea of iteratively refining the context and response using a CoT enhanced RTRA cycle is a valuable contribution. However, the individual components aren't groundbreaking in isolation; it's the specific configuration tailored for medical VQA that demonstrates potential.  The claim of being the first multimodal RAG framework for MedVQA seems plausible, although a thorough review of the literature would be needed to confirm this definitively.

*   **Significance:** Medical applications of AI demand high reliability and accuracy. The potential for MIRA to reduce hallucinations and improve factual grounding is therefore significant.  The speedup claim is also notable, suggesting practical applicability.  The ablation studies help to justify the design decisions and show the contribution of the various modules.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   *Comprehensive Architecture*: The architecture of the MIRA framework is well-defined, incorporating several key innovations such as the RTRA pipeline and the MRAG strategy. The adaptive attention mechanism allows for balancing image and text, and is beneficial for handling different types of queries.

    *   *Clear Problem Statement*: The paper clearly identifies the key challenges with existing MLLMs in medical settings, such as the tendency to hallucinate and the issues related to retrieval imbalance.
    *   *Solid Experimental Results*: The experiments on MIMIC-CXR and PubMedVision indicate state-of-the-art performance. Comparisons with other existing methods highlight the improvement achieved by MIRA. Also, the attention visualization provides insight into how the framework utilizes the retrieved information.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   *Limited Generalization Discussion*: The paper focuses on radiology and PubMedVision. The introduction states the model is designed to work across a range of medical domains (pathology, dermatology). More experimental validation in diverse medical domains would strengthen the claims of generalizability.
    *   *Implementation Details*: Although the implementation is based on PyTorch, details on the exact training setup and fine-tuning procedures are sparse. While they mention mixed-precision training and hyperparameter optimization, specific details of the search space and optimization strategies are missing. A more detailed appendix could resolve this.
    *   *Limited Discussion of Ethical Implications*: While improving factual accuracy is inherently beneficial, a deeper discussion of the potential biases in the data, the risks of over-reliance on AI in medical decision-making, and the framework's limitations in specific clinical scenarios would be valuable.
    *   *Lack of Comparison with MMed-RAG*: It is acknowledged that MMed-RAG lacks available code and weights, but a further discussion of its architectural differences would be beneficial, along with an explanation of why it was impossible to reimplement the model for comparison.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the results are reproducible and the framework proves adaptable to different medical domains, MIRA could influence the development of more reliable and trustworthy AI systems for medical diagnosis and clinical support. The focus on real-time evidence integration could also drive further research in dynamic RAG systems.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the novel combination of techniques, solid experimental results on standard medical benchmarks, and the potential for impact in a critical application area, but also acknowledging the limitations in generalization discussion and implementation details, a score of **7** is appropriate. While the individual components aren't revolutionary, their integration into a tailored framework for medical VQA constitutes a significant and valuable contribution.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Can Large Language Models Improve Phishing Defense? A Large-Scale Controlled Experiment on Warning Dialogue Explanations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07916v1)
- **Authors**: Federico Maria Cau, Giuseppe Desolda, Francesco Greco, Lucio Davide Spano, Luca Viganò
- **Abstract**: Phishing has become a prominent risk in modern cybersecurity, often used to bypass technological defences by exploiting predictable human behaviour. Warning dialogues are a standard mitigation measure, but the lack of explanatory clarity and static content limits their effectiveness. In this paper, we report on our research to assess the capacity of Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate clear, concise, and scalable explanations for phishing warnings. We carried out a large-scale between-subjects user study (N = 750) to compare the influence of warning dialogues supplemented with manually generated explanations against those generated by two LLMs, Claude 3.5 Sonnet and Llama 3.3 70B. We investigated two explanatory styles (feature-based and counterfactual) for their effects on behavioural metrics (click-through rate) and perceptual outcomes (e.g., trust, risk, clarity). The results indicate that well-constructed LLM-generated explanations can equal or surpass manually crafted explanations in reducing susceptibility to phishing; Claude-generated warnings exhibited particularly robust performance. Feature-based explanations were more effective for genuine phishing attempts, whereas counterfactual explanations diminished false-positive rates. Other variables such as workload, gender, and prior familiarity with warning dialogues significantly moderated warning effectiveness. These results indicate that LLMs can be used to automatically build explanations for warning users against phishing, and that such solutions are scalable, adaptive, and consistent with human-centred values.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates whether Large Language Models (LLMs) can improve phishing defense by generating more effective warning dialogue explanations. The authors conduct a large-scale (N=750) controlled experiment comparing warning dialogues supplemented with manually generated explanations against those generated by two LLMs: Claude 3.5 Sonnet and Llama 3.3 70B.  They also explore two explanatory styles: feature-based and counterfactual. The study analyzes click-through rates (CTR) and user perceptions (trust, risk, clarity). The key findings indicate that LLM-generated explanations, particularly those from Claude, can be as effective as or even surpass manually crafted ones in reducing phishing susceptibility.  Counterfactual explanations are found to be beneficial in reducing false positives, while feature-based explanations are more effective against true phishing attempts.  User characteristics like workload, gender, and prior familiarity significantly moderate warning effectiveness. The authors conclude that LLMs offer a scalable, adaptive, and human-centered approach to automatically generating explanations for phishing warnings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Large-Scale Empirical Validation:** The study's strength lies in its substantial sample size, enhancing the statistical power and generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:** The direct comparison of manually generated explanations with those generated by multiple LLMs (both commercial and open-source), and the investigation of different explanation styles, provides valuable insights.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The research addresses a pressing cybersecurity challenge – phishing – with a solution that is potentially scalable and cost-effective.
    *   **Nuanced Findings:** The identification of context-specific effectiveness (e.g., counterfactual explanations for reducing false positives) offers a more fine-grained understanding of LLM applications in this domain.
    *   **Consideration of User Factors:** The acknowledgement and analysis of user-related moderators enhance the practical applicability of the results.
    *   The prompt engineering process is well-documented.
    *   The study considers the impact of using closed-weight models like Claude for users who do not want to share private data.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **CTR as Sole Behavioral Metric:**  While CTR is a standard metric, it is not always a perfect indicator of understanding or intention, thus limiting the comprehensiveness of findings.
    *   **Ecological Validity Considerations:** The controlled lab setting, even with attempts to mimic real-world scenarios, might not fully capture the complexities of user behavior in natural phishing contexts.
    *   **Limited Generalizability to LLMs:** The study focused on only two specific LLMs. While the choice is justified by their characteristics (commercial vs. open-source), broader claims about LLM capabilities should be interpreted with caution.
    *   **Focus on Explanation Style:** The qualitative insights relating to explanation styles are limited. The analysis could be more thorough in revealing why certain explanation styles are preferred in particular scenarios.
    *   The paper could be more specific on how the LLMs were prompted.
    *   The study only considers 3 different emails.
    *   Despite applying Bonferroni correction, Type I errors could have remained.
    *   The study could have performed an A/B test on the different prompts used to generate the explanations.
    *   Although the paper highlights statistically significant data, it also includes that which is not, which seems contradictory.
*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The paper makes a significant contribution by providing empirical evidence for the use of LLMs in generating explanations for phishing warnings. It moves beyond theoretical possibilities and explores the practical implications of this approach in a controlled setting.  While the general concept of using explanations to mitigate phishing is not entirely new, the rigorous, large-scale evaluation of LLM-generated explanations across different styles and LLMs represents a notable advancement. The identification of the nuanced benefits of different explanation styles and the moderating influence of user factors adds to the novelty and practical value of the study.
*   **Potential Influence:** The findings have the potential to influence the design of future phishing defense systems, particularly in exploring automated explanation generation and personalized warning dialogues. This paper provides a strong foundation for further research on leveraging LLMs to improve cybersecurity awareness and user protection.

**Score:** 7.5/10

**Justification:**

The paper provides a compelling, well-executed empirical evaluation of LLM-generated explanations in the context of phishing defense. The large scale, comparative analysis, and consideration of user factors contribute to its strength and practical relevance. However, the limitations regarding CTR as a metric, generalizability to other LLMs, and the limited exploration of other explanation styles slightly temper its overall impact. A score of 7.5 reflects the paper's valuable contribution and strong potential to influence the field, while acknowledging areas for improvement and further investigation. The paper provides a significant leap in the field and a stepping stone for further LLM integration within Cybersecurity.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Low Resource Reconstruction Attacks Through Benign Prompts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07947v1)
- **Authors**: Sol Yarkoni, Roi Livni
- **Abstract**: The recent advances in generative models such as diffusion models have raised several risks and concerns related to privacy, copyright infringements and data stewardship. To better understand and control the risks, various researchers have created techniques, experiments and attacks that reconstruct images, or part of images, from the training set. While these techniques already establish that data from the training set can be reconstructed, they often rely on high-resources, excess to the training set as well as well-engineered and designed prompts. In this work, we devise a new attack that requires low resources, assumes little to no access to the actual training set, and identifies, seemingly, benign prompts that lead to potentially-risky image reconstruction. This highlights the risk that images might even be reconstructed by an uninformed user and unintentionally. For example, we identified that, with regard to one existing model, the prompt ``blue Unisex T-Shirt'' can generate the face of a real-life human model. Our method builds on an intuition from previous works which leverages domain knowledge and identifies a fundamental vulnerability that stems from the use of scraped data from e-commerce platforms, where templated layouts and images are tied to pattern-like prompts.
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper titled "Low Resource Reconstruction Attacks Through Benign Prompts" addresses the risks associated with generative models, particularly focusing on privacy and data misuse. Previous research in this area typically required extensive resources, access to training sets, and carefully crafted prompts to demonstrate the reconstruction of training data. The authors propose a novel attack mechanism that significantly reduces these resource demands. Their method can reconstruct images using benign prompts with little to no access to the original training datasets. For instance, they illustrate that a simple prompt like "blue Unisex T-Shirt" can lead to the generation of a real human model's face from the training data. The research emphasizes vulnerabilities due to the use of scraped data from e-commerce platforms, where the structured relationship between templates and prompts can inadvertently reveal sensitive information. ### Critical Evaluation **Novelty:** The paper introduces a fresh perspective on the reconstruction attacks landscape by demonstrating that simple and benign prompts can lead to significant privacy violations without requiring extensive resources. This shifts the understanding of how accessible and concerning data reconstruction can be for less informed users, highlighting a fundamental vulnerability in machine learning models trained with publicly available data. **Strengths:** 1. **Practical Implications:** The findings pose real-world risks, particularly for the deployment of generative models in e-commerce and related fields. The idea that benign prompts can inadvertently reveal sensitive information underscores a critical oversight in current data stewardship practices. 2. **Accessibility:** By reducing the resource requirement for such attacks, the research makes it clear that even uninformed users can pose a risk, broadening the scope of users who might exploit these vulnerabilities unintentionally. 3. **E-commerce Vulnerability Analysis:** It effectively identifies a specific vulnerability associated with templated layouts and patterns within scraped data, thereby contributing valuable insights to the conversation on data ethics. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limitations on Generalizability:** While the specific example used to demonstrate the attack is compelling, the study would benefit from exploring a wider range of prompts and models to assess the robustness and generalizability of the findings. 2. **Lack of Mitigation Strategies:** The paper primarily focuses on highlighting the risks without offering substantial strategies or frameworks to mitigate these risks moving forward. 3. **Depth of Experimentation:** A more thorough empirical analysis of various generative models and the efficacy of the proposed attack in different contexts would strengthen the paper further. **Overall Impact:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the understanding of generative model vulnerabilities and privacy issues related to image reconstruction. By focusing on low-resource methods and benign prompts, it captures a concerning trend that demands attention from both researchers and practitioners in the field. Given the significance of the findings, their practical implications, and the call for deeper scrutiny in the usage of generative models within ethically sensitive contexts, I would assign a score of **7**. This score reflects the paper’s innovative approach and its relevance in the ongoing discourse regarding data privacy in the era of advanced generative modeling, while acknowledging gaps in the generalizability of findings and practical recommendations. Score: 7
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Scaling RL to Long Videos
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07966v1)
- **Authors**: Yukang Chen, Wei Huang, Baifeng Shi, Qinghao Hu, Hanrong Ye, Ligeng Zhu, Zhijian Liu, Pavlo Molchanov, Jan Kautz, Xiaojuan Qi, Sifei Liu, Hongxu Yin, Yao Lu, Song Han
- **Abstract**: We introduce a full-stack framework that scales up reasoning in vision-language models (VLMs) to long videos, leveraging reinforcement learning. We address the unique challenges of long video reasoning by integrating three critical components: (1) a large-scale dataset, LongVideo-Reason, comprising 52K long video QA pairs with high-quality reasoning annotations across diverse domains such as sports, games, and vlogs; (2) a two-stage training pipeline that extends VLMs with chain-of-thought supervised fine-tuning (CoT-SFT) and reinforcement learning (RL); and (3) a training infrastructure for long video RL, named Multi-modal Reinforcement Sequence Parallelism (MR-SP), which incorporates sequence parallelism and a vLLM-based engine tailored for long video, using cached video embeddings for efficient rollout and prefilling. In experiments, LongVILA-R1-7B achieves strong performance on long video QA benchmarks such as VideoMME. It also outperforms Video-R1-7B and even matches Gemini-1.5-Pro across temporal reasoning, goal and purpose reasoning, spatial reasoning, and plot reasoning on our LongVideo-Reason-eval benchmark. Notably, our MR-SP system achieves up to 2.1x speedup on long video RL training. LongVILA-R1 demonstrates consistent performance gains as the number of input video frames scales. LongVILA-R1 marks a firm step towards long video reasoning in VLMs. In addition, we release our training system for public availability that supports RL training on various modalities (video, text, and audio), various models (VILA and Qwen series), and even image and video generation models. On a single A100 node (8 GPUs), it supports RL training on hour-long videos (e.g., 3,600 frames / around 256k tokens).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Scaling RL to Long Videos":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LongVILA-R1, a framework designed to scale reasoning in vision-language models (VLMs) for long videos. The approach addresses the challenges of long video understanding by integrating three key components: a new large-scale dataset called LongVideo-Reason containing 52K high-quality question-answering (QA) pairs with reasoning annotations; a two-stage training pipeline that extends VLMs with chain-of-thought supervised fine-tuning (CoT-SFT) and reinforcement learning (RL); and a training infrastructure called Multi-modal Reinforcement Sequence Parallelism (MR-SP), which leverages sequence parallelism and a vLLM-based engine for efficient rollout and prefilling. The authors demonstrate that LongVILA-R1 achieves strong performance on long video QA benchmarks, outperforming existing models including Video-R1 and approaching the performance of Gemini-1.5-Pro on temporal, goal-oriented, spatial, and narrative reasoning tasks. The authors also release the training system for public use.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has several novel aspects:
    *   **LongVideo-Reason Dataset:**  The creation of a large, high-quality dataset specifically designed for long video reasoning is a significant contribution. Existing datasets often lack the scale or quality of reasoning annotations required to train robust models.
    *   **MR-SP Training Infrastructure:** The MR-SP system, which combines sequence parallelism and a vLLM engine with caching, is a novel approach to addressing the computational challenges of RL training for long videos. It enables training on much longer videos than previously possible and achieves significant speedups.
    *   **Integration of CoT-SFT and RL:**  The two-stage training pipeline, which combines supervised fine-tuning with chain-of-thought reasoning and RL, is a promising approach for improving the reasoning capabilities of VLMs.

*   **Significance:**
    *   **Advancing Long Video Understanding:**  The paper tackles a critical problem in the field of VLMs: the ability to reason about events and relationships over extended periods in videos. The proposed framework represents a significant step forward in addressing this challenge.
    *   **Enabling New Applications:** By improving long video understanding, LongVILA-R1 has the potential to enable new applications in areas such as embodied AI, robotics, autonomous systems, and video analytics.
    *   **Open-Source Release:**  The release of the training system and dataset is a valuable contribution to the community and will facilitate further research in this area.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** The paper presents a well-integrated framework that addresses multiple aspects of long video reasoning, from data creation to training infrastructure.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of LongVILA-R1 on a variety of benchmarks, showcasing its superiority over existing models.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:**  The paper provides a detailed analysis of the different components of the framework, including the design of the LongVideo-Reason dataset and the MR-SP training system.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** Training LongVILA-R1 still requires significant computational resources. While the MR-SP system improves efficiency, it would be valuable to explore further optimizations.
    *   **Generalization:** While LongVILA-R1 performs well on the benchmarks considered in the paper, it is important to assess its generalization capabilities on a wider range of long video reasoning tasks.
    *   **Dataset Bias:** As with any dataset, the LongVideo-Reason dataset may contain biases that could affect the performance of trained models. It would be useful to analyze the dataset for potential biases and develop strategies to mitigate their impact.
    *   **Qualitative Results:** While the inclusion of qualitative results provides more insights and context, a more rigorous methodology of evaluating the reasoning, such as A/B tests or human studies, could strengthen the claims of superior reasoning.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper represents a significant advance in long video understanding for VLMs. The combination of a new high-quality dataset, a novel training infrastructure, and a promising training pipeline make it a valuable contribution to the field. However, the computational cost and potential dataset bias warrant further investigation.

Considering the overall novelty and significance, balanced against the outlined weaknesses, a rigorous, critical assessment yields the following score:

**Score: 7.5**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Geometry Forcing: Marrying Video Diffusion and 3D Representation for Consistent World Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07982v1)
- **Authors**: Haoyu Wu, Diankun Wu, Tianyu He, Junliang Guo, Yang Ye, Yueqi Duan, Jiang Bian
- **Abstract**: Videos inherently represent 2D projections of a dynamic 3D world. However, our analysis suggests that video diffusion models trained solely on raw video data often fail to capture meaningful geometric-aware structure in their learned representations. To bridge this gap between video diffusion models and the underlying 3D nature of the physical world, we propose Geometry Forcing, a simple yet effective method that encourages video diffusion models to internalize latent 3D representations. Our key insight is to guide the model's intermediate representations toward geometry-aware structure by aligning them with features from a pretrained geometric foundation model. To this end, we introduce two complementary alignment objectives: Angular Alignment, which enforces directional consistency via cosine similarity, and Scale Alignment, which preserves scale-related information by regressing unnormalized geometric features from normalized diffusion representation. We evaluate Geometry Forcing on both camera view-conditioned and action-conditioned video generation tasks. Experimental results demonstrate that our method substantially improves visual quality and 3D consistency over the baseline methods. Project page: https://GeometryForcing.github.io.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Geometry Forcing: Marrying Video Diffusion and 3D Representation for Consistent World Modeling" addresses the problem that video diffusion models often lack a strong understanding of 3D geometry, leading to inconsistencies in generated videos.  The authors propose "Geometry Forcing" (GF), a method that encourages video diffusion models to learn latent 3D representations. This is achieved by aligning intermediate representations of the diffusion model with features extracted from a pre-trained 3D foundation model (VGGT). The alignment is enforced using two complementary objectives: Angular Alignment (directional consistency) and Scale Alignment (preservation of scale-related information). Experiments on camera view-conditioned and action-conditioned video generation tasks demonstrate that GF improves visual quality and 3D consistency compared to baseline methods. The paper also demonstrates the ability to reconstruct explicit geometry from the intermediate features, opening up possibilities for structured memory and long-term world modeling.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of aligning video diffusion model features with a pre-trained 3D foundation model to inject geometric awareness is reasonably novel. The specific implementation, using Angular and Scale Alignment objectives, is also a contribution. However, the concept of representation alignment, drawing inspiration from REPA for image diffusion models, reduces the absolute novelty somewhat. The use of a pre-trained 3D model, while practical, does limit novelty compared to end-to-end training. The authors convincingly demonstrate that simply training on video data isn't enough and propose a straightforward approach to address this limitation. However, other approaches to incorporate geometric constraints have been explored, diminishing the paper's uniqueness.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem in video generation: the lack of 3D consistency. GF provides a practical method to improve this, leading to more realistic and coherent videos. The empirical results are compelling, showing substantial gains in visual quality and 3D consistency metrics. The ability to reconstruct 3D geometry from the diffusion model's features is also a promising development with potential for downstream applications. This work could influence future research on video generation and world modeling by highlighting the importance of incorporating 3D geometric priors. The clear writing style and thorough experimental evaluation make it a valuable contribution. The limitations are well articulated.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses a clear and important problem.
    *   Presents a relatively simple and effective method.
    *   Provides strong empirical evidence supporting the claims.
    *   Explores interesting downstream applications.
    *   Well-written and easy to understand.
    *   Thorough ablations to justify design choices

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Relies on a pre-trained 3D foundation model, which limits end-to-end learnability and potentially generalizability if the 3D foundation model has biases.
    *   The individual components (representation alignment, angular/scale loss) aren't entirely novel in isolation, although their specific combination in this context is.
    *   The paper focuses on improving *consistency* and *geometry*, but lacks a discussion on the potential trade-offs with diversity or creativity in video generation.
    *   The limitations of the method regarding scalability to very large models and datasets are acknowledged, indicating a potential constraint on its broader applicability.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research in video generation by emphasizing the importance of incorporating 3D geometric information. The proposed GF method provides a practical and effective way to achieve this, which could inspire other researchers to explore similar techniques. The demonstration of 3D reconstruction capabilities from diffusion models is also a valuable contribution that could pave the way for new applications in world modeling and interactive environments.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a reasonably novel and significant contribution to the field of video generation by proposing Geometry Forcing to improve 3D consistency. The method is effective, well-evaluated, and addresses a relevant problem. However, the reliance on a pre-trained 3D foundation model and the incremental nature of some components prevent it from achieving a higher score. While not groundbreaking, the work represents a solid advancement and is likely to stimulate further research in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Performance and Practical Considerations of Large and Small Language Models in Clinical Decision Support in Rheumatology
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07983v1)
- **Authors**: Sabine Felde, Rüdiger Buchkremer, Gamal Chehab, Christian Thielscher, Jörg HW Distler, Matthias Schneider, Jutta G. Richter
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) show promise for supporting clinical decision-making in complex fields such as rheumatology. Our evaluation shows that smaller language models (SLMs), combined with retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), achieve higher diagnostic and therapeutic performance than larger models, while requiring substantially less energy and enabling cost-efficient, local deployment. These features are attractive for resource-limited healthcare. However, expert oversight remains essential, as no model consistently reached specialist-level accuracy in rheumatology.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the performance of large language models (LLMs) and smaller language models (SLMs) for clinical decision support in rheumatology. It compares five state-of-the-art language models (GPT-4o, Mixtral-8x7b-32768, Nemotron (Llama-3.1-70b-instruct), Qwen-Turbo 2.5, and Claude-3.5-Sonnet) with and without Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG). The study evaluates the models based on F1 scores for diagnostic and therapeutic accuracy and RAGAS (Retrieval-Augmented Generation Assessment Score) for factual alignment and relevance. The results suggest that SLMs, particularly Mixtral-8x7b-32768 with RAG, can achieve high performance in both diagnostic and therapeutic tasks, potentially offering a cost-effective alternative to larger models, especially in resource-constrained settings. However, the paper emphasizes the need for expert oversight as no model consistently reached specialist-level accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Practical Focus:** The paper addresses a practical problem - the use of LLMs/SLMs for clinical decision support, specifically in rheumatology, a field known for its diagnostic challenges.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:** It offers a direct comparison of several popular language models, providing insights into their relative strengths and weaknesses.
    *   **RAG Integration:** The inclusion of RAG is significant because it acknowledges and attempts to mitigate the limitations of static knowledge in LLMs by integrating current, external knowledge.
    *   **SLM Emphasis:** Focusing on SLMs is valuable. The paper identifies the potential benefits of smaller, more deployable and energy-efficient models, which is crucial for resource-limited healthcare environments.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** The use of F1 scores and RAGAS provides a quantitative assessment of model performance.
    *   **Real-World Data:** The models were tested using anonymized clinical records, enhancing the relevance and applicability of the study.
    *   **Clear Limitations:** The authors acknowledge limitations, including the small sample size, reliance on specific metrics, and potential for prompt engineering to influence results.
    *   **Importance of Expertise:** The study correctly underscores the critical role of expert oversight when applying AI-driven decision support.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Small Sample Size:** The ten patient cases constitute a small sample size, which might limit the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Specificity of Rheumatology:** The results are specific to rheumatology, and their applicability to other medical domains needs further investigation.
    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** The evaluation focuses primarily on diagnostic and treatment accuracy based on guideline-based ground truths. Other clinically relevant aspects, such as nuanced treatment considerations or patient-specific factors, are not fully explored.
    *   **General RAG implementation details:** The paper mentions FAISS, sentence embeddings, and flash rank, but it lacks details of how those specific features were used. This information is important to evaluate the RAG quality.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The novelty lies in the comparative analysis of different LLMs/SLMs, including recent models, for a specific medical domain (rheumatology) with a focus on smaller models and the integration of RAG. While the application of LLMs in medicine is not entirely new, the direct comparison and practical emphasis on deployable SLMs contribute to the field. The significance lies in providing guidance for healthcare professionals considering the use of AI for decision support, especially in resource-constrained settings.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    The paper has the potential to influence future research by encouraging further exploration of SLMs for medical applications and the development of more sophisticated RAG techniques that account for nuanced clinical factors. It can also inform healthcare institutions about the potential benefits and limitations of different AI models, helping them make informed decisions about implementation.

*   **Score Rationale**

The paper addresses a relevant topic and provides a useful comparison of LLMs and SLMs in the medical domain of rheumatology. The findings are well-supported by quantitative data and the limitations are acknowledged. The paper presents a comparative analysis of different LLMs and SLMs in Rheumatology with RAG, including recent models, focusing on smaller models and the integration of RAG. This focus on a specialized area is significant, and the emphasis on smaller, more deployable models is practical and relevant to real-world healthcare settings. Therefore, the paper demonstrates a level of novelty in the application of these approaches and the comparative evaluation of different models to support decision-making in healthcare. The results of the analysis are clear and well-defined, and the limitations of the study are openly presented.
However, the main limitations are that the study has a limited scope that cannot be generalized and the study has a very small sample of the dataset.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### OST-Bench: Evaluating the Capabilities of MLLMs in Online Spatio-temporal Scene Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07984v1)
- **Authors**: JingLi Lin, Chenming Zhu, Runsen Xu, Xiaohan Mao, Xihui Liu, Tai Wang, Jiangmiao Pang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have shown remarkable capabilities in integrating vision and language for complex reasoning. While most existing benchmarks evaluate models under offline settings with a fixed set of pre-recorded inputs, we introduce OST-Bench, a benchmark designed to evaluate Online Spatio-Temporal understanding from the perspective of an agent actively exploring a scene. The Online aspect emphasizes the need to process and reason over incrementally acquired observations, while the Spatio-Temporal component requires integrating current visual inputs with historical memory to support dynamic spatial reasoning. OST-Bench better reflects the challenges of real-world embodied perception. Built on an efficient data collection pipeline, OST-Bench consists of 1.4k scenes and 10k question-answer pairs collected from ScanNet, Matterport3D, and ARKitScenes. We evaluate several leading MLLMs on OST-Bench and observe that they fall short on tasks requiring complex spatio-temporal reasoning. Under the online setting, their accuracy declines as the exploration horizon extends and the memory grows. Through further experimental analysis, we identify common error patterns across models and find that both complex clue-based spatial reasoning demands and long-term memory retrieval requirements significantly drop model performance along two separate axes, highlighting the core challenges that must be addressed to improve online embodied reasoning. To foster further research and development in the field, our codes, dataset, and benchmark are available. Our project page is: https://rbler1234.github.io/OSTBench.github.io/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "OST-Bench: Evaluating the Capabilities of MLLMs in Online Spatio-temporal Scene Understanding":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OST-Bench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the online spatio-temporal scene understanding capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs).  Unlike existing benchmarks that typically use fixed sets of pre-recorded inputs, OST-Bench challenges models to process and reason about incrementally acquired observations from an agent's perspective as it actively explores a static indoor environment.  The benchmark emphasizes the need for MLLMs to integrate current visual inputs with historical memory to support dynamic spatial reasoning.  OST-Bench comprises 1.4k scenes and 10k question-answer pairs derived from ScanNet, Matterport3D, and ARKitScenes. The authors evaluate several leading MLLMs on OST-Bench, revealing that they struggle with complex spatio-temporal reasoning tasks and experience a decline in accuracy as the exploration horizon and memory demands increase.  The authors identify a "Spatio-temporal Reasoning Shortcut" phenomenon, where models rely on shallow inferences rather than retrieving relevant information from long-term memory.  They also demonstrate that performance degrades along two axes: complex clue-based spatial reasoning and long-term memory retrieval.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The primary novelty lies in the **online** and **agent-centric** nature of the benchmark.  Existing benchmarks largely focus on offline processing or third-person perspectives. Shifting the focus to an embodied agent actively exploring and constructing a representation of its environment is a significant and valuable contribution. The emphasis on *online* scene understanding and integration with historical memory distinguishes it from many existing spatial reasoning benchmarks. The authors effectively leverage existing datasets (ScanNet, Matterport3D, ARKitScenes) to create a new benchmark with a unique focus.
*   **Significance:**  The significance stems from the benchmark's ability to expose limitations in current MLLMs regarding spatio-temporal reasoning, particularly within an embodied agent context. By evaluating leading models, the authors highlight specific challenges that need to be addressed, such as long-term memory retrieval and complex spatial inference. These limitations are important to address because they will limit the potential for these models in real-world tasks. The benchmark's design and comprehensive evaluation provide a valuable resource for researchers working on embodied AI and visual-language reasoning. The identification of the "Spatio-temporal Reasoning Shortcut" is also a significant contribution, shedding light on a common failure mode in these models.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined task:** The paper clearly articulates the task of online spatio-temporal scene understanding and provides a rigorous benchmark for evaluating models.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The authors evaluate a range of leading MLLMs, including both proprietary and open-source models. The detailed analysis of performance across different question types and exploration horizons provides valuable insights.
    *   **Error Analysis:** The identification of error patterns, such as Spatio-temporal Reasoning Shortcut, sheds light on the limitations of existing MLLMs and provides directions for future research.
    *   **Public availability:** The planned release of the benchmark and code is a major strength, enabling other researchers to use and extend the work.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Static Environment:** A primary limitation is the focus on a static environment. Real-world embodied agents often interact with and modify their environment. Future work could extend OST-Bench to include dynamic environments.
    *   **Limited Embodied Tasks:** While the benchmark is designed from an embodied agent perspective, it doesn't explicitly evaluate interactive behaviors or manipulation skills. The evaluated capabilities mostly focus on passively perceiving and understanding the scene.
    *   **Question generation:** It is rule-based, as the authors admit. This may not represent all the questions humans would ask, or require answers that humans would give. Future research should explore data collection methods for question-generation tasks.
    *   **Scalability:** It might be computationally expensive to run complex neural networks for the number of runs required by the benchmark. This may hinder future research.

*   **Potential Influence:**  OST-Bench is likely to become a valuable benchmark for the community, driving research in areas such as:
    *   Improving long-term memory in MLLMs.
    *   Developing more robust spatio-temporal reasoning capabilities.
    *   Creating models better suited for embodied AI tasks.
    *   Exploring mechanisms for dynamically distilling and retaining knowledge during exploration.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and significant benchmark that addresses a key gap in evaluating MLLMs for embodied AI. While there are some limitations, the strengths of the benchmark in exposing the flaws of current models in online scene understanding are important. In addition, the error analysis and the identification of Spatio-temporal Reasoning Shortcuts are valuable. The publicly available benchmark is likely to drive further innovation in this area, and therefore it is a well-executed, significant contribution.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Automating Expert-Level Medical Reasoning Evaluation of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07988v1)
- **Authors**: Shuang Zhou, Wenya Xie, Jiaxi Li, Zaifu Zhan, Meijia Song, Han Yang, Cheyenna Espinoza, Lindsay Welton, Xinnie Mai, Yanwei Jin, Zidu Xu, Yuen-Hei Chung, Yiyun Xing, Meng-Han Tsai, Emma Schaffer, Yucheng Shi, Ninghao Liu, Zirui Liu, Rui Zhang
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) become increasingly integrated into clinical decision-making, ensuring transparent and trustworthy reasoning is essential. However, existing evaluation strategies of LLMs' medical reasoning capability either suffer from unsatisfactory assessment or poor scalability, and a rigorous benchmark remains lacking. To address this, we introduce MedThink-Bench, a benchmark designed for rigorous, explainable, and scalable assessment of LLMs' medical reasoning. MedThink-Bench comprises 500 challenging questions across ten medical domains, each annotated with expert-crafted step-by-step rationales. Building on this, we propose LLM-w-Ref, a novel evaluation framework that leverages fine-grained rationales and LLM-as-a-Judge mechanisms to assess intermediate reasoning with expert-level fidelity while maintaining scalability. Experiments show that LLM-w-Ref exhibits a strong positive correlation with expert judgments. Benchmarking twelve state-of-the-art LLMs, we find that smaller models (e.g., MedGemma-27B) can surpass larger proprietary counterparts (e.g., OpenAI-o3). Overall, MedThink-Bench offers a foundational tool for evaluating LLMs' medical reasoning, advancing their safe and responsible deployment in clinical practice.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MedThink-Bench, a new benchmark dataset designed for rigorously evaluating the medical reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs). The dataset comprises 500 complex medical questions across ten domains, each meticulously annotated with expert-crafted, step-by-step rationales.  To leverage this benchmark, the authors propose LLM-w-Ref, a novel evaluation framework that combines fine-grained expert rationales with an LLM-as-a-Judge mechanism.  LLM-w-Ref aims to provide a more accurate and scalable assessment of LLMs' medical reasoning by evaluating the intermediate reasoning steps. The authors benchmarked twelve state-of-the-art LLMs using MedThink-Bench and LLM-w-Ref, demonstrating the framework's strong correlation with expert judgments and identifying instances where smaller, open-source models outperform larger, proprietary ones. The paper emphasizes the importance of transparent and trustworthy medical reasoning in LLMs, highlighting the limitations of existing evaluation strategies and advocating for the use of their proposed benchmark and framework for safe and responsible deployment in clinical practice.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant contribution by addressing the limitations of current LLM medical reasoning evaluation methods. While LLM-as-a-Judge and text-similarity metrics have been used before, the combination of a *high-quality, expert-annotated dataset* (MedThink-Bench) with a *refined LLM-as-a-Judge framework* (LLM-w-Ref) makes the approach novel. The focus on *step-by-step rationale assessment* to identify flawed reasoning is a valuable addition. The finding that smaller, open-source models can outperform larger, proprietary ones is an important empirical observation.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to improve the reliability and trustworthiness of LLMs used in medical contexts. The ability to rigorously evaluate and identify flawed reasoning is crucial for safe deployment in clinical practice. MedThink-Bench offers a standardized benchmark that can be used by researchers to compare and improve LLM medical reasoning capabilities. The LLM-w-Ref framework provides a practical approach to scalable and expert-aligned evaluation, addressing a key challenge in the field. This also makes research in explainability in LLM medicine more robust.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **High-Quality Dataset:** The expert-annotated dataset (MedThink-Bench) is a significant asset, addressing a major limitation of previous evaluation approaches that rely on LLM-generated rationales.
    *   **Novel Evaluation Framework:** LLM-w-Ref offers a practical approach to scalable and expert-aligned evaluation by refining and building upon LLM-as-a-Judge methods.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The thorough benchmarking of twelve state-of-the-art LLMs provides valuable empirical evidence and demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed framework.
    *   **Focus on Transparent Reasoning:** The emphasis on evaluating the underlying reasoning process, rather than just prediction accuracy, is crucial for building trustworthy medical AI.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Size:** While high-quality, the size of MedThink-Bench (500 questions) is still relatively limited compared to some other datasets, potentially affecting the generalizability of the results.
    *   **Potential Data Leakage:** The use of questions derived from existing literature raises the possibility of some data leakage, although the authors acknowledge this limitation. This is a pervasive issue in the field, and it does not invalidate the work.
    *   **Reliance on LLM Judge:** LLM-w-Ref still relies on an LLM for evaluation, thus, there remains inherent biases of the judge model.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of medical AI by providing a valuable benchmark and evaluation framework. MedThink-Bench could become a widely used resource for comparing and improving LLM medical reasoning capabilities. LLM-w-Ref offers a practical approach to addressing the challenges of scalability and expert alignment in evaluation, potentially leading to more reliable and trustworthy medical AI systems.

*   **Score Justification:** While the paper has some minor limitations, the novelty and significance of the proposed benchmark and evaluation framework are considerable. The strengths of the expert-annotated dataset, rigorous benchmarking, and focus on transparent reasoning outweigh the weaknesses. The paper addresses a critical need in the field and offers a valuable tool for promoting safe and responsible deployment of LLMs in medical contexts.

**Score: 8**

**Reason:** The paper constitutes a strong contribution to the field of medical AI, addressing a critical gap in reliable and scalable evaluation methods. While there exist data leakage and small size limitations in dataset (as noted in weaknesses), the novelty and significance of high-quality benchmark data outweighs the limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Multi-Granular Spatio-Temporal Token Merging for Training-Free Acceleration of Video LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07990v1)
- **Authors**: Jeongseok Hyun, Sukjun Hwang, Su Ho Han, Taeoh Kim, Inwoong Lee, Dongyoon Wee, Joon-Young Lee, Seon Joo Kim, Minho Shim
- **Abstract**: Video large language models (LLMs) achieve strong video understanding by leveraging a large number of spatio-temporal tokens, but suffer from quadratic computational scaling with token count. To address this, we propose a training-free spatio-temporal token merging method, named STTM. Our key insight is to exploit local spatial and temporal redundancy in video data which has been overlooked in prior work. STTM first transforms each frame into multi-granular spatial tokens using a coarse-to-fine search over a quadtree structure, then performs directed pairwise merging across the temporal dimension. This decomposed merging approach outperforms existing token reduction methods across six video QA benchmarks. Notably, STTM achieves a 2$\times$ speed-up with only a 0.5% accuracy drop under a 50% token budget, and a 3$\times$ speed-up with just a 2% drop under a 30% budget. Moreover, STTM is query-agnostic, allowing KV cache reuse across different questions for the same video. The project page is available at https://www.jshyun.me/projects/sttm.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the provided paper titled "Multi-Granular Spatio-Temporal Token Merging for Training-Free Acceleration of Video LLMs". Here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary**

The paper introduces STTM, a training-free method for accelerating video Large Language Models (LLMs). STTM tackles the computational bottleneck caused by the quadratic scaling of attention with the number of spatio-temporal tokens in video data. It achieves this by exploiting local spatial and temporal redundancy within video content. The method first transforms each frame into multi-granular spatial tokens using a coarse-to-fine search via a quadtree structure. It then performs directed pairwise merging of tokens across the temporal dimension. Experiments on various video QA benchmarks demonstrate that STTM outperforms existing token reduction methods and achieves significant speed-ups with minimal accuracy loss, while also enabling KV cache reuse across different queries. The method claims to be query-agnostic, which is a significant benefit for multi-turn interactions.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of exploiting *both* spatial and temporal redundancy in a *training-free* manner for video LLMs is reasonably novel. While existing methods addressed either spatial or temporal redundancy (or did it with training), the combination and specific implementation using a quadtree and directed temporal merging presents a unique approach. The main novelty lies in the combined approach that leverages spatio-temporal characteristics within video data, and specifically by focusing on a training-free methodology.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in deploying video LLMs: the computational cost associated with long video sequences. The ability to reduce the number of tokens significantly, while maintaining reasonable accuracy and enabling KV cache reuse, is a valuable contribution that could make video LLMs more practical for real-world applications. The performance improvements, especially in scenarios with long videos, are significant. The query-agnostic nature of STTM is also important, as it allows for caching and efficient multi-turn conversations. The generalization ability of the method (showing improvements across different LLMs) is another strong aspect.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Training-Free:** Avoiding the need for retraining makes STTM applicable to existing models.
    *   **Spatio-Temporal Consideration:** Exploiting both dimensions is more effective than single-dimension approaches.
    *   **Multi-Granularity:** The use of a quadtree allows for adaptive token reduction, preserving detail where needed.
    *   **Query-Agnostic:** Enables KV cache reuse, which is very efficient.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** STTM demonstrably outperforms existing methods across multiple datasets and LLMs.
    *   **Comprehensive Ablation Studies:** Thoroughly validates the effectiveness of the different components of STTM.
    *   The visualizations help in intuitively understanding how the method operates.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Parameter Tuning:** The method relies on manually adjusted threshold values (Ts and TT). The paper acknowledges that the adjustment is emperical. Automated or adaptive threshold selection would be a significant improvement. The current emperical threshold tuning reduces the "training-free" claim somewhat, as careful configuration is needed for different models or datasets.
    *   **Complexity of Implementation:** While the paper explains the method clearly, the implementation involving quadtrees, union-find, and careful merging is not necessarily trivial.
    *   The current evaluation is limited to QA tasks. While this is a common task, evaluating on other video understanding tasks might be helpful.
    *   While efficient, the computational complexity of the quadtree process, although *linear*, could be a bottleneck for very high-resolution videos. The implementation details related to parallelization are not thoroughly presented.
    * The results can be affected by the inherent quality of the base LLM model used for testing.
    * The ablation studies demonstrate the contribution of various components, however, the overall analysis could be enhanced by exploring combinations of the existing token reduction methods with the proposed approach.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by shifting the focus towards training-free, spatio-temporal token merging techniques. It provides a practical and effective approach that researchers can build upon. The KV cache reuse aspect is particularly valuable and could be adopted more widely.

**Justification for Score**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign a score of **7**.

*   The method is demonstrably effective and addresses a real problem.
*   The training-free aspect, while somewhat mitigated by the manual parameter tuning, is a significant advantage.
*   The combination of spatial and temporal considerations is innovative.
*   The query-agnostic nature and demonstration of KV-cache reuse are important.
*   However, the dependence on manually tuned parameters, the implementation complexity, and the focus on only QA tasks prevent it from reaching a higher score. Adaptive threshold selection, further exploration of task diversity and improved explanation of parallel implementation would significantly strengthen the work.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Multigranular Evaluation for Brain Visual Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07993v1)
- **Authors**: Weihao Xia, Cengiz Oztireli
- **Abstract**: Existing evaluation protocols for brain visual decoding predominantly rely on coarse metrics that obscure inter-model differences, lack neuroscientific foundation, and fail to capture fine-grained visual distinctions. To address these limitations, we introduce BASIC, a unified, multigranular evaluation framework that jointly quantifies structural fidelity, inferential alignment, and contextual coherence between decoded and ground truth images. For the structural level, we introduce a hierarchical suite of segmentation-based metrics, including foreground, semantic, instance, and component masks, anchored in granularity-aware correspondence across mask structures. For the semantic level, we extract structured scene representations encompassing objects, attributes, and relationships using multimodal large language models, enabling detailed, scalable, and context-rich comparisons with ground-truth stimuli. We benchmark a diverse set of visual decoding methods across multiple stimulus-neuroimaging datasets within this unified evaluation framework. Together, these criteria provide a more discriminative, interpretable, and comprehensive foundation for measuring brain visual decoding methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces BASIC, a new framework for evaluating brain visual decoding methods. Recognizing the limitations of existing coarse metrics that lack neuroscientific grounding and fail to capture fine-grained visual distinctions, BASIC offers a unified, multigranular approach. This framework quantifies structural fidelity, inferential alignment, and contextual coherence between decoded and ground truth images.  It introduces hierarchical segmentation-based metrics for structural evaluation and leverages multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to extract structured scene representations (objects, attributes, relationships) for semantic evaluation.  The paper benchmarks a diverse set of visual decoding methods across multiple stimulus-neuroimaging datasets using this framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the comprehensive and multigranular approach to evaluation. Instead of relying on simple pixel-wise or feature-based similarities, BASIC attempts to capture the hierarchical nature of visual perception, aligning the evaluation more closely with how humans process visual information. The use of MLLMs for semantic analysis is a significant contribution, allowing for scalable and context-rich comparisons.
    *   **Weakness:** While the individual components (segmentation metrics, LLMs) are not entirely novel, the *integration* into a cohesive evaluation framework is. The novelty of the overall framework depends on the specific contributions (e.g. the way in which LLMs are prompted, the specific segmentation metrics used etc).
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in the brain visual decoding field: the lack of robust and interpretable evaluation metrics. By offering a more discriminative and neuroscientifically-grounded evaluation framework, BASIC has the potential to improve the rigor and comparability of brain decoding research. It allows for a more nuanced understanding of what specific aspects of visual information are being successfully decoded, and where current methods are falling short.
    *   **Weakness:** The practical impact of BASIC depends on its adoption by the research community. If researchers continue to rely on traditional metrics, the potential influence of BASIC will be limited. There needs to be an organized effort to encourage adoption, like creating a software package which is easily installed.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive:** BASIC provides a more complete picture of decoding performance compared to existing metrics.
    *   **Interpretable:**  The framework offers diagnostic information, allowing researchers to identify specific errors in the decoding process.
    *   **Neuroscientifically grounded:** The metrics are designed to align with principles of human visual perception.
    *   **Versatile:** Demonstrated applicability across multiple stimulus types (image, video, 3D) and neuroimaging modalities.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:** The framework is complex, involving multiple steps and potentially requiring significant computational resources, particularly with the use of MLLMs.
    *   **Reliance on MLLMs:** The reliance on MLLMs introduces potential biases and hallucinations from these models. While the paper attempts to mitigate this, it remains a factor. The results are influenced by the choices that were made, so they are not objective.
    *   **Weighted Scores:** The weights assigned to different components (e.g., object, attribute, relation in BASIC-H) could influence the overall evaluation, requiring careful justification and potentially sensitivity analysis.
    *   **Limited comparison with ground truth (Human):** Though the authors did correct LLM's output with the help of human experts, the BASIC is still using LLM as ground truth. For the brain decoding problem, a key point is whether the reconstruction matches how the subject perceived the image, which might be very different to what the LLM says.
*   **Potential Influence:** BASIC could significantly influence the field by:
    *   Driving the development of brain decoding methods that are more aligned with human visual perception.
    *   Facilitating more meaningful comparisons between different decoding methods.
    *   Identifying specific weaknesses in current methods, guiding future research efforts.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a significant advancement in evaluation methodology for brain visual decoding. While the framework is complex and has limitations related to its reliance on MLLMs and the need for careful parameter tuning, it offers a far more nuanced and interpretable assessment than existing approaches. Its potential to improve the rigor and comparability of research in the field is substantial.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10
